This is **three passes in one session, in a fixed order**, and the order is load-bearing. Each pass
is allowed to see strictly more than the one before it. You cannot un-read a spec, so the pass that
must judge the code cold goes first.

1. Work the passes **in the order written**. Do not read ahead. Do not open a file a later pass
   grants you until you reach that pass. In particular: **the story spec is deliberately NOT in the
   diff.** Do not go looking for it during the earlier passes. The final pass will hand you the path.
2. **Freeze each pass before advancing.** Write that pass's findings out in full, to
   `specs/stories/code-review/issue-1154-eqc3-codex-findings.md`, before you open anything the next
   pass allows. Do not revise an earlier pass's findings in light of what a later pass taught you - if

   commands you ran, and anything you could not run. Do not paper over a gap - see "Honesty" below.

## PASS 1 - BLIND HUNTER (the diff, and nothing else)

You get the diff at `specs/stories/code-review/issue-1154-eqc3-diff.txt` and **nothing else**. No
spec, no story file, no project context. Do not explore the repository. Do not go looking for the
spec.

### What this diff claims to be

`POST /api/characters/:id/equipment` gains optional `container_id` support: if present, it must be a
24-hex ObjectId-shaped string, must match the `catalogue_id` of ANOTHER equipment row this SAME
character already owns, and that referenced catalogue item's `bucket` must be `'container'` - all
three checks running BEFORE any write, rejecting the whole request on failure rather than a partial
write. `equipment-derivation.js` gains a new exported pure function, `equipmentContainerLabel(item,
allEquipment, catalogueLookup)`, computing an "(in: <name>)" display string - deliberately checking
whether the CHARACTER's own equipment array still contains a row matching `container_id` (not just
whether the catalogue item globally exists), because a catalogue item can outlive a specific
character's own possession of it. `editor/sheet.js` gains a "Place inside" dropdown in the add-item
form (only rendered when the character owns at least one container) and wires `equipmentContainerLabel`
into six render sections. `editor/edit.js`'s `shAddEquip()` reads the new dropdown and includes
`container_id` in the POST body.

**That is the shape it claims. Do not trust the shape - verify it.**

### What to hunt for

1. **Does the new `container_id` validation block accidentally make `container_id` REQUIRED**, or
   otherwise change behaviour for a request that never mentions it at all? Trace the exact branch
   condition guarding the new checks (is it `if (item.container_id != null)` or something looser that
   could misfire on e.g. an empty string, `0`, or `false`?).
2. **Order of operations**: does the diff's own claimed order (shape -> existence -> bucket-
   correctness, all before the actual `$push` write) actually hold in the code, or does e.g. the
   bucket-correctness DB lookup happen before the cheaper shape check, wasting a round-trip on
   obviously-malformed input? (Low severity if so, but worth noting.)
3. **`equipmentContainerLabel`'s self-reference guard** (`e !== item`): is this REFERENCE equality
   (`!==`) doing real work, or could two distinct object literals with identical content both be
   "the same item" in a way this guard fails to catch (e.g. if the array is ever rebuilt/cloned
   between when `item` was captured and when `allEquipment` is passed in)? Trace where `containedLabel`
   is actually called from in `sheet.js` and confirm `item` and the elements of `allEquipment` are
   genuinely the same object references, not copies.
4. **`shAddEquip()`'s new `containerId` read**: `document.getElementById('eq-add-container')?.value ||
   null` - does this correctly distinguish "the dropdown doesn't exist in the DOM at all" (character
   owns no containers) from "the dropdown exists but nothing is selected" (both should send `null`)?
   Trace both cases.
5. **Trivially-satisfiable assertions** in the new tests - anything that would pass even if the
   underlying logic were wrong.
6. **Self-contradiction**: does any comment claim behaviour the code doesn't implement (e.g. claiming
   the existence check queries the database, when it might actually only check an in-memory array
   already fetched)?
7. **Unused imports / dead code**: is `equipmentContainerLabel` actually imported and called in
   `sheet.js`, or could the import exist without the call site (or vice versa - called without being
   imported, a ReferenceError)?

**STOP. Write your Pass 1 findings to
`specs/stories/code-review/issue-1154-eqc3-codex-findings.md` now, before reading further.**

## PASS 2 - EDGE CASE HUNTER (the diff, plus the repository)

You now have full read access to `D:\Terra Mortis\TM Suite-eqc` (this worktree only - see Ground
Rules). You still do **not** have the story spec or any account of the author's intent.

### Orientation (not ground truth - verify against the code)

Same summary as Pass 1: `container_id` write-path validation added to an existing, live equipment-add
endpoint; a new pure display predicate checking character-scoped (not catalogue-global) container
ownership; a new UI picker; wiring through the existing add-item client function.

### What to hunt for

1. **Hand-trace the FULL write-path validation** in `server/routes/characters.js` for FOUR scenarios:
   (a) no `container_id` at all (must behave EXACTLY as before this diff), (b) `container_id` present
   but malformed (not 24-hex), (c) `container_id` well-formed but referencing a catalogue_id this
   character has never added, (d) `container_id` well-formed and owned, but that catalogue item's
   `bucket` is NOT `'container'` (e.g. it's `'combat_gear'`). Confirm each produces exactly the
   response the diff's comments/messages claim.
2. **Read `server/schemas/character.schema.js`'s `container_id` field definition** (not part of this
   diff, but load-bearing context) and confirm the new route code's validation is actually CONSISTENT
   with what the schema itself permits - does the schema's own pattern match what the route checks, or
   could the route accept something the schema would reject at a different write path (or vice versa)?
3. **Race/ordering concern**: the existence check reads `char.equipment` from a `findOne` BEFORE the
   `$push` that adds the new item. Could a concurrent second request (adding a DIFFERENT item with a
   `container_id` pointing at a container the FIRST request is simultaneously adding) pass validation
   against a stale read? Is this a realistic concern for a single-ST-admin-at-a-time tool, or worth
   flagging as a real (if narrow) race?
4. **`equipmentContainerLabel`'s catalogue lookup fallback**: if `containerEntry` resolves (the
   character still owns a row with this catalogue_id) but that catalogue entry's `name` field is
   itself null/empty, does the function fall back sensibly (the diff claims `containerEntry.name ||
   item.container_id`) - trace this exactly.
5. **Grep the WHOLE repo** for any OTHER write path that could set a character's `equipment[]` array
   (e.g. a bulk `PUT /:id` character-replace route) - does THAT route need the same `container_id`
   validation this diff added only to `POST /:id/equipment`, or is `POST` genuinely the only equipment-
   adding write path? If there's a second path, is it now inconsistently validated?
6. **The "Place inside" dropdown's option values**: read `editor/sheet.js`'s render code and confirm
   the `<option value="...">` uses the SAME string form (`item.catalogue_id`, presumably already a
   string) that `server/routes/characters.js`'s validation later compares `container_id` against - any
   type mismatch (e.g. one side ObjectId-ish, the other a raw string with different casing) would make
   every dropdown selection fail validation.

**STOP. Write your Pass 2 findings to
`specs/stories/code-review/issue-1154-eqc3-codex-findings.md` now, before reading further.**

+/**
+ * EQC-3 (issue #1154, epic #1038) - "(in: <container name>)" display label
+ * for a contained item. Checks the CHARACTER's own equipment array for
+ * another row still carrying this `container_id` as its `catalogue_id` -
+ * NOT just whether the catalogue item globally exists. A container removed
+ * from this character's own equipment (its row deleted via DELETE
+ * /:id/equipment/:itemIndex) must render its former contents as loose, even
+ * though the catalogue item itself may still exist for OTHER characters (the
+ * catalogue-admin delete guard only blocks deleting a CATALOGUE item while
+ * ANY character holds it - it does not know about container_id references
+ * at all). This is EQC-1's own "display-inert on dangling reference"
+ * contract, applied correctly.
+ *
+ * Extracted as its own exported pure function (not a closure inside
+ * editor/sheet.js's shRenderEquipment) so it is directly unit-testable,
+ * matching this module's established convention.
+ *
+ * @param {object} item - a character's equipment[] entry (has `container_id`)
+ * @param {object[]} allEquipment - the SAME character's full equipment[] array
+ * @param {function} [catalogueLookup] - (id) => catalogue entry | undefined
+ * @returns {string|null} `in: <name>` | null
+ */
+export function equipmentContainerLabel(item, allEquipment, catalogueLookup = getCatalogueEntry) {
+  if (!item || !item.container_id) return null;
+  const list = Array.isArray(allEquipment) ? allEquipment : [];
+  const stillOwned = list.some(e => e && e !== item && String(e.catalogue_id) === item.container_id);
+  if (!stillOwned) return null;
+  const containerEntry = catalogueLookup(item.container_id);
+  return containerEntry ? `in: ${containerEntry.name || item.container_id}` : null;
+}

+// ── POST /:id/equipment — container_id (EQC-3, issue #1154) ─────────────────
+//
+// container_id was introduced by EQC-1 (#1152) but never validated or even
+// accepted by this route until now — this is the first real consumer.

+describe('POST /api/characters/:id/equipment — container_id validation (EQC-3, #1154)', () => {
+  it('null/absent container_id — unchanged behaviour, stored as null', async () => {
+    const c = await seedChar({ name: 'EQC-3 no container' });
+    const res = await request(app)
+      .post(`/api/characters/${c.id}/equipment`)
+      .set('X-Test-User', stUser())
+      .send({ catalogue_id: String(catKnife._id), state: 'carried', acquired_cycle: 1 });
+    expect(res.status).toBe(200);
+    expect(res.body.equipment[0].container_id).toBeNull();
+  });
+
+  it('valid container_id — accepted and stored as a string, referencing an already-owned container row', async () => {
+    const c = await seedChar({ name: 'EQC-3 with container' });
+    // First, the character must already own the container itself.
+    await request(app).post(`/api/characters/${c.id}/equipment`)
+      .set('X-Test-User', stUser())
+      .send({ catalogue_id: String(catHaven._id), state: 'active', acquired_cycle: 0 });
+    // Now place a second item inside it.
+    const res = await request(app)
+      .post(`/api/characters/${c.id}/equipment`)
+      .set('X-Test-User', stUser())
+      .send({ catalogue_id: String(catKnife._id), state: 'stashed', acquired_cycle: 1, container_id: String(catHaven._id) });
+    expect(res.status).toBe(200);
+    const placed = res.body.equipment.find(e => String(e.catalogue_id) === String(catKnife._id));
+    expect(placed.container_id).toBe(String(catHaven._id));
+    // Stored as a plain string, not coerced to an ObjectId (unlike catalogue_id).
+    const stored = await getCollection('characters').findOne({ _id: c._id }, { projection: { equipment: 1 } });
+    const storedItem = stored.equipment.find(e => String(e.catalogue_id) === String(catKnife._id));
+    expect(typeof storedItem.container_id).toBe('string');
+  });
+
+  it('400 when container_id is not a 24-hex string', async () => {
+    const c = await seedChar({ name: 'EQC-3 malformed container' });
+    const res = await request(app)
+      .post(`/api/characters/${c.id}/equipment`)
+      .set('X-Test-User', stUser())
+      .send({ catalogue_id: String(catKnife._id), state: 'carried', acquired_cycle: 1, container_id: 'not-a-valid-id' });
+    expect(res.status).toBe(400);
+    expect(res.body.error).toBe('VALIDATION_ERROR');
+    expect(res.body.message).toMatch(/container_id must be a 24-hex ObjectId string/);
+  });
+

+# Adversarial review findings — issue #1154 / EQC-3
+
+## High
+
+- [Pass 1] None found.
+
+## Medium
+
+### [Pass 1] Uppercase 24-hex `container_id` values are rejected despite satisfying the stated shape
+
+- **Severity:** Medium
+- **File:line:** `server/routes/characters.js:879`
+- **Triggering input or sequence:** Send an otherwise-valid equipment-add request with `container_id` equal to an already-owned container catalogue ID rendered in uppercase hexadecimal, for example `ABCDEF0123456789ABCDEF01`.
+- **Observable consequence:** `ObjectId.isValid()` accepts the value, but `String(new ObjectId(value))` canonicalizes it to lowercase, so the strict equality check returns 400. That conflicts with the diff's stated contract of accepting a “24-hex ObjectId-shaped string,” for which uppercase hex is ordinarily shape-valid.
+- **Confidence:** Medium-high from the diff alone; Pass 2 must check whether the schema deliberately narrows the field to lowercase.
+
+## Low
+
+### [Pass 1] The self-reference guard relies on object identity and its test only exercises the favorable identity case
+
+- **Severity:** Low
+- **File:line:** `public/js/data/equipment-derivation.js:148`; `server/tests/issue-879-defence-penalty-wirein.test.js:578`
+- **Triggering input or sequence:** Call `equipmentContainerLabel()` with `item` as a clone/rebuilt object while `allEquipment` contains the original row with the same `catalogue_id`, where that ID also equals `item.container_id`.
+- **Observable consequence:** `e !== item` is true for the distinct objects, so the only row can be treated as “another” owned row and the item can be labelled as contained in itself. The new test uses `[item]`, so it cannot discriminate reference identity from stable row identity. Whether the production call can supply clones is deferred to Pass 2 repository tracing.
+- **Confidence:** High that the helper has this behavior; confidence that the current UI can trigger it is intentionally deferred until Pass 2.
+
+### [Pass 1] Rejection tests do not prove the advertised all-before-write behavior
+
+- **Severity:** Low
+- **File:line:** `server/tests/equipment.test.js:266`
+- **Triggering input or sequence:** Introduce a regression that pushes the requested equipment row and then returns one of the tested 400 responses for malformed, unowned, or non-container `container_id`.
+- **Observable consequence:** All three new rejection tests still pass because they assert only status/error/message and never reread the character to prove its equipment array stayed unchanged. The displayed route implementation writes only after validation, but the tests do not protect that load-bearing property against later refactoring.
+- **Confidence:** High.
+

    1: /**
    2:  * equipment-derivation.js — armour penalty + derived-defence materialisation.
    3:  *
    4:  * Issue #879 (ADR-006). Lifts EQ-1's "calcDefence does not read the equipment
    5:  * array without an ADR" gate. ADR-006 specifies:
    6:  *
    7:  *   D1: armourDefencePenalty(c, catalogueLookup?) reads c.equipment[] filtered
    8:  *       by state === 'worn' + bucket === 'combat_gear' with isCombatGearArmourShaped(entry);
    9:  *       injectable catalogue lookup (default: the ECM-5 cache reader).
   10:  *
   11:  *       EQC-1 (issue #1152, epic #1038, 2026-08-13): the old `armour` bucket
   12:  *       merged into `combat_gear` (which also covers weapons). "Armour-shaped"
   13:  *       is identified by `isCombatGearArmourShaped` (armour_value OR
   14:  *       defence_penalty populated - see that function's own comment for why
   15:  *       a single-field check was wrong) rather than by bucket alone — the
   16:  *       epic's own "distinct stat fields" distinction within the single
   17:  *       combat_gear bucket.
   18:  *   D2: worst-case math — Math.max(...penalties). The editor surfaces a soft
   19:  *       hint when >1 armour is worn (wording is concern #8 below).
   20:  *   D2-FLOOR: floor at 0 lives ONLY at the helper composition site. STM overlay
   21:  *       composes additively on top per ADR-004's no-bounds contract; the
   22:  *       sheet renderer adds NO further clamp.
   23:  *   D3: composition order — calcDefence(c) → subtract helper → floor → applyStMods.
   24:  *   D4: STM overlay composes on top of c.derived.defence (the armour-adjusted
   25:  *       base materialised by this module's materialiseDerivedDefence).
   26:  *   D5: pure helper, symmetric with discAttrBonus.
   27:  *
   28:  * Concern #2 from ADR-006: the per-item armour annotation in editor/sheet.js:2240
   29:  * must KEEP calling raw calcDefence(c). Its display intent is "if you wore only
   30:  * this item, defence would be X" — a hypothetical pre-armour baseline, NOT the
   31:  * live armour-adjusted value. Don't migrate that read site.
   32:  *
   33:  * Concern #9 from ADR-006: NO redundant floor clamps anywhere else (not in
   34:  * applyStMods, not in the sheet renderer). The clamp lives in exactly one
   35:  * place — the materialisation call below.
   36:  */
   37: 
   38: import { calcDefence } from './accessors.js';
   39: import { getCatalogueEntry } from './equipment-catalogue-cache.js';
   40: // #896: meritEffectiveRating respects free dots / cp / xp / bonus channels.
   41: // Used to detect Resources cap and Fixer presence without rewriting the
   42: // accessor chain.
   43: import { meritEffectiveRating } from '../editor/domain.js';
   44: 
   45: /**
   46:  * EQC-1 review patch (issue #1152, Codex external review, 2026-08-13,
   47:  * Pass 3b HIGH finding): the FIRST version of these predicates checked only
   48:  * `armour_value != null` / `weapon_type != null`. That is too narrow - under
   49:  * the OLD (pre-EQC-1) schema, bucket-specific fields were independently
   50:  * nullable and never required as a set (ECM-1's own Non-Goal: "no per-bucket
   51:  * schema validation"). A real legacy armour item could have `armour_value:
   52:  * null` while still carrying a populated `defence_penalty`, or a legacy
   53:  * weapon could have `weapon_type: null` while carrying `damage_mod`. A
   54:  * single-field check silently drops such an item from armour/weapon
   55:  * derivation the moment it is migrated to `combat_gear`, even though the
   56:  * migration script reports success - the exact defect the review caught by
   57:  * direct execution (`armour_value: null, defence_penalty: 2` -> penalty 0
   58:  * instead of 2 after migration).
   59:  *
   60:  * These two predicates are the SINGLE SOURCE OF TRUTH for "is this
   61:  * combat_gear entry armour-shaped / weapon-shaped" - every consumer
   62:  * (armourDefencePenalty/wornArmourCount below, roll.js, roll-v2.js,
   63:  * editor/sheet.js) imports and uses these rather than re-deriving its own
   64:  * copy, so the discriminator can never drift out of sync between consumers
   65:  * again the way it did in the first version of this story.
   66:  */
   67: export function isCombatGearArmourShaped(entry) {
   68:   if (!entry) return false;
   69:   return entry.armour_value != null || entry.defence_penalty != null;
   70: }
   71: 
   72: export function isCombatGearWeaponShaped(entry) {
   73:   if (!entry) return false;
   74:   return entry.weapon_type != null || entry.damage_mod != null || entry.damage_type != null;
   75: }
   76: 
   77: /**
   78:  * EQC-2 (issue #1153, epic #1038) - "on me" (bonus applies in game) vs
   79:  * "owned but elsewhere" (available in downtime only), per the epic's own
   80:  * display distinction. Derived from the EXISTING `state` field, not a new
   81:  * stored value - see the story's own Background for why a second
   82:  * independent field was rejected (same "two things that can disagree" class
   83:  * of bug the combat_gear shape check above needed fixing for).
   84:  *
   85:  * `carried`/`worn`/`active` = physically on the character = on me.
   86:  * `stashed` = owned but elsewhere. `lost` = neither (the item is gone).
   87:  *
   88:  * NOTE: "on me" (possession) is BROADER than "bonus currently active" for
   89:  * armour specifically, which additionally requires `state === 'worn'` (see
   90:  * armourDefencePenalty above) - a carried-but-unworn breastplate is on you
   91:  * but grants no AR. This predicate answers "is it near me right now", not
   92:  * "is every possible bonus from it firing" - callers that need the latter
   93:  * (armour) keep their own narrower check unchanged.
   94:  *
   95:  * Single source of truth for every consumer that previously re-derived this
   96:  * exact three-state check inline (roll.js, roll-v2.js) - see EQC-1's own
   97:  * isCombatGearArmourShaped/isCombatGearWeaponShaped for why duplicating this
   98:  * kind of check per-consumer is the mistake to avoid.
   99:  *
  100:  * @param {object} item - a character's equipment[] entry (has `state`)
  101:  * @returns {boolean}
  102:  */
  103: export function isEquipmentOnMe(item) {
  104:   if (!item) return false;
  105:   return item.state === 'carried' || item.state === 'worn' || item.state === 'active';
  106: }
  107: 
  108: /**
  109:  * EQC-2 review patch (issue #1153, Codex external review Low finding):
  110:  * the player-facing "On you" / "Stored elsewhere" text label. The first
  111:  * version of this treated ANY non-'lost' state as "elsewhere", including a
  112:  * missing/malformed/unrecognised `state` value - a confident but
  113:  * unsupported location claim for bad data. Only `'stashed'` is a genuinely
  114:  * known "elsewhere" state; anything else that isn't on-me returns `null`
  115:  * (no label) rather than guessing.
  116:  *
  117:  * Extracted as its own exported pure function (not left inline in
  118:  * editor/sheet.js) so it is directly unit-testable, matching this module's
  119:  * own "single shared predicate, not duplicated/embedded inline" convention.
  120:  *
  121:  * @param {object} item - a character's equipment[] entry (has `state`)
  122:  * @returns {string|null} 'On you' | 'Stored elsewhere' | null
  123:  */
  124: export function equipmentLocationLabel(item) {
  125:   if (isEquipmentOnMe(item)) return 'On you';
  126:   if (item && item.state === 'stashed') return 'Stored elsewhere';
  127:   return null;
  128: }
  129: 
  130: /**
  131:  * EQC-3 (issue #1154, epic #1038) - "(in: <container name>)" display label
  132:  * for a contained item. Checks the CHARACTER's own equipment array for
  133:  * another row still carrying this `container_id` as its `catalogue_id` -
  134:  * NOT just whether the catalogue item globally exists. A container removed
  135:  * from this character's own equipment (its row deleted via DELETE
  136:  * /:id/equipment/:itemIndex) must render its former contents as loose, even
  137:  * though the catalogue item itself may still exist for OTHER characters (the
  138:  * catalogue-admin delete guard only blocks deleting a CATALOGUE item while
  139:  * ANY character holds it - it does not know about container_id references
  140:  * at all). This is EQC-1's own "display-inert on dangling reference"
  141:  * contract, applied correctly.
  142:  *
  143:  * Extracted as its own exported pure function (not a closure inside
  144:  * editor/sheet.js's shRenderEquipment) so it is directly unit-testable,
  145:  * matching this module's established convention.
  146:  *
  147:  * @param {object} item - a character's equipment[] entry (has `container_id`)
  148:  * @param {object[]} allEquipment - the SAME character's full equipment[] array
  149:  * @param {function} [catalogueLookup] - (id) => catalogue entry | undefined
  150:  * @returns {string|null} `in: <name>` | null
  151:  */
  152: export function equipmentContainerLabel(item, allEquipment, catalogueLookup = getCatalogueEntry) {
  153:   if (!item || !item.container_id) return null;
  154:   const list = Array.isArray(allEquipment) ? allEquipment : [];
  155:   const stillOwned = list.some(e => e && e !== item && String(e.catalogue_id) === item.container_id);
  156:   if (!stillOwned) return null;
  157:   const containerEntry = catalogueLookup(item.container_id);
  158:   return containerEntry ? `in: ${containerEntry.name || item.container_id}` : null;
  159: }
  160: 
  161: /**
  162:  * Sum-by-worst-case of defence penalties from currently-worn armour.
  163:  * Returns a non-negative integer (the magnitude — composition site subtracts).
  164:  *
  165:  * Per ADR-006 D2: worst-case stacking. Math.max of all qualifying penalties;
  166:  * 0 when no qualifying armour is worn. Negative defence_penalty values are
  167:  * non-sensical for armour and are filtered out before combination.
  168:  *
  169:  * Per ADR-006 D1: state === 'worn' is the only state that contributes. The
  170:  * positive predicate is preferred over a not-stashed/not-lost shape because
  171:  * a future state-enum addition would silently affect a negative predicate
  172:  * (Concern #3).
  173:  *
  174:  * @param {object} c - character document
  175:  * @param {function} [catalogueLookup] - (id) => catalogue entry | undefined.
  176:  *   Default: the ECM-5 cache reader. Tests inject a synthetic map.
  177:  * @returns {number} non-negative integer penalty magnitude
  178:  */
  179: export function armourDefencePenalty(c, catalogueLookup = getCatalogueEntry) {
  180:   if (!c || !Array.isArray(c.equipment)) return 0;
  181:   const penalties = [];
  182:   for (const item of c.equipment) {
  183:     if (!item || item.state !== 'worn') continue;
  184:     const entry = catalogueLookup(item.catalogue_id);
  185:     if (!entry || entry.bucket !== 'combat_gear' || !isCombatGearArmourShaped(entry)) continue;
  186:     const p = Number.isInteger(entry.defence_penalty) ? entry.defence_penalty : 0;
  187:     if (p > 0) penalties.push(p);
  188:   }
  189:   if (penalties.length === 0) return 0;
  190:   return Math.max(...penalties);
  191: }
  192: 
  193: /**
  194:  * Count of armour items currently in state==='worn'. Drives the editor hint
  195:  * surfaced when >1 armour is worn (ADR-006 D2 + Concern #8 — wording verbatim
  196:  * in editor/sheet.js armour-section header).
  197:  *
  198:  * @param {object} c - character document
  199:  * @param {function} [catalogueLookup]
  200:  * @returns {number} count of worn armour items
  201:  */
  202: export function wornArmourCount(c, catalogueLookup = getCatalogueEntry) {
  203:   if (!c || !Array.isArray(c.equipment)) return 0;
  204:   let n = 0;
  205:   for (const item of c.equipment) {
  206:     if (!item || item.state !== 'worn') continue;
  207:     const entry = catalogueLookup(item.catalogue_id);
  208:     if (entry?.bucket === 'combat_gear' && isCombatGearArmourShaped(entry)) n++;
  209:   }
  210:   return n;
  211: }
  212: 
  213: /**
  214:  * Compute and materialise `c.derived.defence` per ADR-006 D3 + D4.
  215:  *
  216:  *   c.derived.defence = max(0, calcDefence(c) - armourDefencePenalty(c))
  217:  *
  218:  * This is the armour-adjusted base. applyStMods (called AFTER this, per the
  219:  * render-path orchestrator) reads `c.derived.defence` as base, applies the
  220:  * STM delta on top, and writes back — no longer the pre-ADR-006 bug where
  221:  * applyStMods treated missing base as 0 (so STM mods on derived.defence
  222:  * silently composed against 0 instead of the real mechanical base).
  223:  *
  224:  * Per ADR-006 D2-FLOOR + Concern #9: floor lives HERE, only HERE. No
  225:  * defensive clamp in applyStMods or the sheet renderer.
  226:  *
  227:  * @param {object} c - character document (mutated)
  228:  * @param {function} [catalogueLookup]
  229:  * @returns {number} the materialised armour-adjusted defence (pre-overlay)
  230:  */
  231: export function materialiseDerivedDefence(c, catalogueLookup = getCatalogueEntry) {
  232:   if (!c) return 0;
  233:   const base = calcDefence(c);
  234:   const penalty = armourDefencePenalty(c, catalogueLookup);
  235:   const adjusted = Math.max(0, base - penalty);
  236:   c.derived = c.derived || {};
  237:   c.derived.defence = adjusted;
  238:   return adjusted;
  239: }
  240: 
  241: /**
  242:  * Read-site helper: returns the modded c.derived.defence when it's been
  243:  * materialised (the common case post-boot per the D8 cache-entry invariant);
  244:  * falls back to a fresh armour-adjusted computation when not materialised
  245:  * (edge cases — fresh chars, test contexts). The fallback is the same
  246:  * computation materialiseDerivedDefence performs; the difference is whether
  247:  * the result is cached on c.derived.defence or computed on the fly.
  248:  *
  249:  * Used by sheet.js, suite/sheet.js, roll calc, DT player pool, DT admin
  250:  * resolution view — every consumer that previously called calcDefence(c)
  251:  * directly migrates here.
  252:  *
  253:  * @param {object} c - character document
  254:  * @param {function} [catalogueLookup]
  255:  * @returns {number} displayed defence (overlay-modded if applicable)
  256:  */
  257: export function defenceForDisplay(c, catalogueLookup = getCatalogueEntry) {
  258:   if (c && c.derived && typeof c.derived.defence === 'number') return c.derived.defence;
  259:   if (!c) return 0;
  260:   return Math.max(0, calcDefence(c) - armourDefencePenalty(c, catalogueLookup));
  261: }
  262: 
  263: /**
  264:  * Export-site helper: canonical mechanical defence (armour-adjusted, no
  265:  * STM overlay). Always computes fresh — does NOT read c.derived.defence
  266:  * which may carry an overlay-modded value at export time.
  267:  *
  268:  * Per ADR-004: STM overlays are scene-only adjustments and NOT canonical
  269:  * character state. Character export (JSON / CSV / snapshot) captures the
  270:  * mechanical baseline; overlays are a separate concern.
  271:  *
  272:  * @param {object} c - character document
  273:  * @param {function} [catalogueLookup]
  274:  * @returns {number} mechanical defence baseline (armour-adjusted)
  275:  */
  276: export function defenceMechanicalBase(c, catalogueLookup = getCatalogueEntry) {
  277:   if (!c) return 0;
  278:   return Math.max(0, calcDefence(c) - armourDefencePenalty(c, catalogueLookup));
  279: }
  280: 
  281: // ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  282: // #896 — Equipment availability filter + Fixer errata (Peter 2026-06-18).
  283: //
  284: // Composition is simple subtraction with no overlay/scene-state interaction
  285: // (per Khepri dispatch — no ADR needed). The helpers below are pure: take
  286: // (item, c) → number / boolean. Read sites use them at render time; DT form
  287: // uses them to gate dropdown affordability.
  288: //
  289: // Fixer errata: the 2-dot Social merit Fixer reduces the availability cost
  290: // of all items by 1. Applies in all usage and displays — dropdown, sheet
  291: // held-items, admin editor row labels (the admin editor BYPASSES the
  292: // affordability gate but still SHOWS the reduced number for consistency).
  293: //
  294: // Out of scope (Peter / Khepri): admin catalogue page (ECM-6) shows raw
  295: // catalogue availability; it is character-agnostic.
  296: // ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  297: 
  298: /**
  299:  * Resources cap = effective rating of the character's Resources merit, or 0
  300:  * if absent. Uses meritEffectiveRating so cp / xp / free / bonus channels
  301:  * compose correctly without re-walking the merit array.
  302:  *
  303:  * @param {object} c - character document
  304:  * @returns {number} resources rating (0-5)
  305:  */
  306: export function availabilityCap(c) {
  307:   if (!c) return 0;
  308:   const m = (c.merits || []).find(x => x?.name === 'Resources');
  309:   return m ? meritEffectiveRating(c, m) : 0;
  310: }
  311: 
  312: /**
  313:  * Fixer reduction = 1 if the character has Fixer merit with effective rating
  314:  * >= 1, else 0. The Fixer merit is a fixed 2-dot Social merit per the rules,
  315:  * but we gate on the effective-rating check rather than mere presence so a
  316:  * suppressed / zeroed Fixer entry (edge case during merit editing) doesn't
  317:  * spuriously grant the reduction.
  318:  *
  319:  * @param {object} c - character document
  320:  * @returns {0 | 1} reduction magnitude
  321:  */
  322: export function fixerReduction(c) {
  323:   if (!c) return 0;
  324:   const m = (c.merits || []).find(x => x?.name === 'Fixer');
  325:   if (!m) return 0;
  326:   return meritEffectiveRating(c, m) >= 1 ? 1 : 0;
  327: }
  328: 
  329: /**
  330:  * Effective availability of a catalogue item for a specific character:
  331:  *
  332:  *   effectiveAvailability(item, c) = max(0, item.availability - fixerReduction(c))
  333:  *
  334:  * Floored at 0 so a level-0 item with Fixer doesn't surface as -1; the
  335:  * floor lives here, no clamp needed at consumer sites (same single-floor
  336:  * discipline as ADR-006 Concern #9).
  337:  *
  338:  * `item` may be a catalogue entry (post-ECM-1 shape with `availability` int
  339:  * field) OR a partial; null/undefined item or missing availability defaults
  340:  * to 0.
  341:  *
  342:  * @param {object} item - catalogue entry
  343:  * @param {object} c - character document
  344:  * @returns {number} effective availability (0-5)
  345:  */
  346: export function effectiveAvailability(item, c) {
  347:   const raw = Number.isInteger(item?.availability) ? item.availability : 0;
  348:   return Math.max(0, raw - fixerReduction(c));
  349: }
  350: 
  351: /**
  352:  * Convenience wrapper: is the item within the character's affordability gate?
  353:  *
  354:  *   isAffordable(item, c) = effectiveAvailability(item, c) <= availabilityCap(c)
  355:  *
  356:  * Used by the DT form dropdown to disable unaffordable options. The admin
  357:  * character editor explicitly BYPASSES this gate (ST override) but still
  358:  * displays the effective availability per Peter's dispatch.
  359:  *
  360:  * @param {object} item - catalogue entry
  361:  * @param {object} c - character document
  362:  * @returns {boolean}
  363:  */
  364: export function isAffordable(item, c) {
  365:   return effectiveAvailability(item, c) <= availabilityCap(c);
  366: }

  840: 
  841: // POST /api/characters/:id/equipment — append a single equipment item.
  842: //
  843: // ECM-3 (#870): `catalogue_id` is a 24-hex ObjectId string on the wire,
  844: // matching the new equipment_catalogue collection's _id shape. Existence
  845: // is checked via a Mongo lookup against equipment_catalogue, not against
  846: // the static EQUIPMENT_CATALOGUE slug set — the slug set is dead post-ECM-3
  847: // (ECM-7 deletes the static module entirely). The catalogue_id is hydrated
  848: // to an ObjectId before $push so storage stays ObjectId-typed across the
  849: // full lifecycle; see specs/epic-ecm-equipment-catalogue-migration.md.
  850: router.post('/:id/equipment', requireRole('st'), async (req, res) => {
  851:   const oid = parseId(req.params.id);
  852:   if (!oid) return res.status(400).json({ error: 'VALIDATION_ERROR', message: 'Invalid character ID' });
  853: 
  854:   const item = req.body;
  855:   const VALID_STATES = ['carried', 'worn', 'stashed', 'lost', 'active'];
  856:   if (!item || typeof item !== 'object') {
  857:     return res.status(400).json({ error: 'VALIDATION_ERROR', message: 'Request body must be an equipment item object' });
  858:   }
  859:   if (!item.catalogue_id || typeof item.catalogue_id !== 'string') {
  860:     return res.status(400).json({ error: 'VALIDATION_ERROR', message: 'catalogue_id is required' });
  861:   }
  862:   if (!ObjectId.isValid(item.catalogue_id) || String(new ObjectId(item.catalogue_id)) !== item.catalogue_id) {
  863:     return res.status(400).json({
  864:       error: 'VALIDATION_ERROR',
  865:       message: `catalogue_id must be a 24-hex ObjectId string; got '${item.catalogue_id}'`,
  866:     });
  867:   }
  868:   if (!VALID_STATES.includes(item.state)) {
  869:     return res.status(400).json({ error: 'VALIDATION_ERROR', message: `state must be one of: ${VALID_STATES.join(', ')}` });
  870:   }
  871:   if (!Number.isInteger(item.acquired_cycle) || item.acquired_cycle < 0) {
  872:     return res.status(400).json({ error: 'VALIDATION_ERROR', message: 'acquired_cycle must be a non-negative integer' });
  873:   }
  874:   // EQC-3 (issue #1154, epic #1038): container_id — optional, shape check
  875:   // only here (cheap, no DB round-trip). Existence + bucket-correctness are
  876:   // checked further down once `char` is fetched, per this story's own
  877:   // "malformed shape first, then existence, then bucket-correctness"
  878:   // ordering (Dev Notes). EQC-1 introduced this field on the schema but
  879:   // NEVER validated it anywhere - this route is the first real consumer.
  880:   const hasContainerId = item.container_id != null;
  881:   if (hasContainerId) {
  882:     if (typeof item.container_id !== 'string' || !ObjectId.isValid(item.container_id) || String(new ObjectId(item.container_id)) !== item.container_id) {
  883:       return res.status(400).json({
  884:         error: 'VALIDATION_ERROR',
  885:         message: `container_id must be a 24-hex ObjectId string or null; got '${item.container_id}'`,
  886:       });
  887:     }
  888:   }
  889: 
  890:   const catalogueOid = new ObjectId(item.catalogue_id);
  891:   const catalogueDoc = await getCollection('equipment_catalogue').findOne(
  892:     { _id: catalogueOid },
  893:     { projection: { _id: 1 } }
  894:   );
  895:   if (!catalogueDoc) {
  896:     return res.status(404).json({ error: 'NOT_FOUND', message: `Unknown catalogue item: ${item.catalogue_id}` });
  897:   }
  898: 
  899:   // EQC-3: widened from { _id: 1 } to also read `equipment` — needed to
  900:   // check the container_id existence claim below (does this character
  901:   // already own a row with this catalogue_id).
  902:   const char = await col().findOne({ _id: oid }, { projection: { equipment: 1 } });
  903:   if (!char) return res.status(404).json({ error: 'NOT_FOUND', message: 'Character not found' });
  904: 
  905:   if (hasContainerId) {
  906:     // Existence: the character must already own ANOTHER equipment row whose
  907:     // catalogue_id equals container_id. NOTE (known, documented limitation -
  908:     // see the story's own "Explicitly NOT this story"): this cannot
  909:     // distinguish two rows that happen to share the same catalogue_id (e.g.
  910:     // two identical havens) - "does this character own ONE such row" is all
  911:     // this check can prove.
  912:     const ownsContainerCatalogueId = (char.equipment || []).some(
  913:       e => e && String(e.catalogue_id) === item.container_id
  914:     );
  915:     if (!ownsContainerCatalogueId) {
  916:       return res.status(400).json({
  917:         error: 'VALIDATION_ERROR',
  918:         message: `container_id does not reference an item this character already owns: ${item.container_id}`,
  919:       });
  920:     }
  921:     // Bucket-correctness: that catalogue item must actually be a container.
  922:     const containerCatalogueDoc = await getCollection('equipment_catalogue').findOne(
  923:       { _id: new ObjectId(item.container_id) },
  924:       { projection: { bucket: 1 } }
  925:     );
  926:     if (!containerCatalogueDoc || containerCatalogueDoc.bucket !== 'container') {
  927:       return res.status(400).json({
  928:         error: 'VALIDATION_ERROR',
  929:         message: `container_id must reference a container-bucket catalogue item; got bucket '${containerCatalogueDoc?.bucket ?? '(unknown)'}'`,
  930:       });
  931:     }
  932:   }
  933: 
  934:   const cleanItem = {
  935:     catalogue_id:   catalogueOid,
  936:     state:          item.state,
  937:     acquired_cycle: item.acquired_cycle,
  938:     notes:          item.notes ?? null,
  939:     // EQC-3: stored as a plain STRING, not coerced to an ObjectId like
  940:     // catalogue_id is — container_id's own schema type is a string pattern
  941:     // field, not an ObjectId reference type, per EQC-1's own schema comment.
  942:     // Coercing it here would create a type mismatch against the
  943:     // string-comparison read sites (e.g. `e.catalogue_id === containerId`).
  944:     container_id:   hasContainerId ? item.container_id : null,
  945:   };
  946:   const result = await col().findOneAndUpdate(
  947:     { _id: oid },
  948:     { $push: { equipment: cleanItem } },
  949:     { returnDocument: 'after', projection: { equipment: 1 } },
  950:   );

+# Adversarial review findings — issue #1154 / EQC-3
+
+## High
+
+- [Pass 1] None found.
+
+## Medium
+
+### [Pass 1] Uppercase 24-hex `container_id` values are rejected despite satisfying the stated shape
+
+- **Severity:** Medium
+- **File:line:** `server/routes/characters.js:879`
+- **Triggering input or sequence:** Send an otherwise-valid equipment-add request with `container_id` equal to an already-owned container catalogue ID rendered in uppercase hexadecimal, for example `ABCDEF0123456789ABCDEF01`.
+- **Observable consequence:** `ObjectId.isValid()` accepts the value, but `String(new ObjectId(value))` canonicalizes it to lowercase, so the strict equality check returns 400. That conflicts with the diff's stated contract of accepting a “24-hex ObjectId-shaped string,” for which uppercase hex is ordinarily shape-valid.
+- **Confidence:** Medium-high from the diff alone; Pass 2 must check whether the schema deliberately narrows the field to lowercase.
+
+## Low
+
+### [Pass 1] The self-reference guard relies on object identity and its test only exercises the favorable identity case
+
+- **Severity:** Low
+- **File:line:** `public/js/data/equipment-derivation.js:148`; `server/tests/issue-879-defence-penalty-wirein.test.js:578`
+- **Triggering input or sequence:** Call `equipmentContainerLabel()` with `item` as a clone/rebuilt object while `allEquipment` contains the original row with the same `catalogue_id`, where that ID also equals `item.container_id`.
+- **Observable consequence:** `e !== item` is true for the distinct objects, so the only row can be treated as “another” owned row and the item can be labelled as contained in itself. The new test uses `[item]`, so it cannot discriminate reference identity from stable row identity. Whether the production call can supply clones is deferred to Pass 2 repository tracing.
+- **Confidence:** High that the helper has this behavior; confidence that the current UI can trigger it is intentionally deferred until Pass 2.
+
+### [Pass 1] Rejection tests do not prove the advertised all-before-write behavior
+
+- **Severity:** Low
+- **File:line:** `server/tests/equipment.test.js:266`
+- **Triggering input or sequence:** Introduce a regression that pushes the requested equipment row and then returns one of the tested 400 responses for malformed, unowned, or non-container `container_id`.
+- **Observable consequence:** All three new rejection tests still pass because they assert only status/error/message and never reread the character to prove its equipment array stayed unchanged. The displayed route implementation writes only after validation, but the tests do not protect that load-bearing property against later refactoring.
+- **Confidence:** High.
+

 2567: // ── Equipment renderer (EQ-2, issue #656) ────────────────────────────────────
 2568: // Renders catalogue-ref equipment[]. All buckets flow through the same array
 2569: // as of 2026-06-19 (character.assets[] retired).
 2570: // Edit mode shows the same view -- equipment is managed via the ST CRUD API (EQ-1).
 2571: //
 2572: // EQC-1 (issue #1152, epic #1038, 2026-08-13): re-partitioned from the old
 2573: // four buckets (weapon/armour/equipment/asset) to five (combat_gear/
 2574: // skill_gear/tool_utility/narrative/container). combat_gear merges the old
 2575: // weapon+armour buckets -- the Weapons/Armour section split below is now
 2576: // driven by which stat fields are POPULATED on the item (weapon_type/
 2577: // damage_mod = weapon-shaped; armour_value/defence_penalty = armour-shaped),
 2578: // not by a bucket comparison, since both shapes now share one bucket value.
 2579: // An item with neither shape populated (an ST entry with no stats filled in
 2580: // yet) falls to "Other Combat Gear" rather than being silently dropped.
 2581: export function shRenderEquipment(c, editMode) {
 2582:   const equip  = c.equipment || [];
 2583:   if (!editMode && !equip.length) return '';
 2584: 
 2585:   const STATE_LABELS = { carried: 'Carried', worn: 'Worn', stashed: 'Stashed', lost: 'Lost', active: 'Active' };
 2586:   const DMGTYPE      = { lethal: 'Lethal', bashing: 'Bashing', aggravated: 'Aggravated' };
 2587:   const WPNTYPE      = { melee: 'Melee', ranged: 'Ranged', thrown: 'Thrown' };
 2588:   const cycleLabel   = n  => n === 0 ? 'Pre-campaign' : `Cycle ${n}`;
 2589:   const stateChip    = st => `<span class="gen-granted-tag-view">${STATE_LABELS[st] || st}</span>`;
 2590:   // EQC-2 (issue #1153, epic #1038): "on me" (bonus applies in game) vs
 2591:   // "owned but elsewhere" (available in downtime only) — the epic's own
 2592:   // display distinction. Rendered as a muted trait-qual fragment (this
 2593:   // renderer's existing convention for secondary info, e.g. item.notes)
 2594:   // rather than a second competing pill beside the state chip.
 2595:   // EQC-2 review patch (#1153, Codex external review Low finding): the
 2596:   // label logic itself now lives in equipmentLocationLabel
 2597:   // (equipment-derivation.js), not a local closure here, so it's directly
 2598:   // unit-testable — this alias just keeps the call sites below unchanged.
 2599:   const locationLabel = equipmentLocationLabel;
 2600:   // EQC-3 (issue #1154, epic #1038): "(in: <container name>)" for a
 2601:   // contained item. Logic lives in equipmentContainerLabel
 2602:   // (equipment-derivation.js), not a local closure here, so it's directly
 2603:   // unit-testable - this alias just keeps the call sites below unchanged.
 2604:   const containedLabel = item => equipmentContainerLabel(item, equip);
 2605: 
 2606:   let h = '<div class="sh-sec"><div class="sh-sec-title">Equipment</div><div class="merit-list">';
 2607: 
 2608:   // Group equipment items by bucket, preserving flat-array index for remove buttons
 2609:   const byBucket = { combat_gear: [], skill_gear: [], tool_utility: [], narrative: [], container: [] };
 2610:   for (let i = 0; i < equip.length; i++) {
 2611:     const item   = equip[i];
 2612:     const entry  = getCatalogueEntry(item.catalogue_id) || {};
 2613:     const bucket = (entry.bucket && byBucket[entry.bucket]) ? entry.bucket : 'skill_gear';
 2614:     byBucket[bucket].push({ item, entry, idx: i });
 2615:   }
 2616: 
 2617:   // combat_gear sub-split by populated stat fields, not bucket (both shapes
 2618:   // now share the combat_gear bucket). An item matching neither shape (no
 2619:   // stats filled in) still renders, under "Other Combat Gear".
 2620:   // EQC-1 review patch (#1152): use the shared predicates from
 2621:   // equipment-derivation.js rather than a locally re-derived copy, so the
 2622:   // weapon/armour discriminator can never drift out of sync between this
 2623:   // renderer and armourDefencePenalty/the roll calculators again.
 2624:   const combatWeapons = byBucket.combat_gear.filter(x => isCombatGearWeaponShaped(x.entry));
 2625:   const combatArmour  = byBucket.combat_gear.filter(x => !isCombatGearWeaponShaped(x.entry) && isCombatGearArmourShaped(x.entry));
 2626:   const combatOther   = byBucket.combat_gear.filter(x => !isCombatGearWeaponShaped(x.entry) && !isCombatGearArmourShaped(x.entry));
 2627: 
 2628:   // ── Weapons ──
 2629:   if (combatWeapons.length) {
 2630:     h += '<div class="sh-sub-title">Weapons</div>';
 2631:     for (const { item, entry, idx } of combatWeapons) {
 2632:       const name  = entry.name || item.catalogue_id;
 2633:       // #896: per-character effective availability (raw - Fixer reduction).
 2634:       const eff = entry.availability != null ? effectiveAvailability(entry, c) : null;
 2635:       const parts = [
 2636:         entry.damage_mod != null ? `+${entry.damage_mod}` : null,
 2637:         DMGTYPE[entry.damage_type] || entry.damage_type || null,
 2638:         WPNTYPE[entry.weapon_type] || entry.weapon_type || null,
 2639:         eff != null ? `avail ${eff}` : null,
 2640:         locationLabel(item),
 2641:         containedLabel(item),
 2642:       ].filter(Boolean);
 2643:       const qual   = parts.join(' · ');
 2644:       const rmBtn  = editMode ? `<button class="sk-spec-rm" style="float:right;margin-top:2px" onclick="shRemoveEquip(${idx})" title="Remove">× Remove</button>` : '';
 2645:       h += `<div class="merit-plain"><div class="trait-row">` +
 2646:         `<div class="trait-main"><span class="trait-name">${esc(name)}</span><div class="trait-right">${stateChip(item.state)}${rmBtn}</div></div>` +
 2647:         `<div class="trait-sub">${qual ? `<span class="trait-qual">${esc(qual)}</span>` : ''}${item.notes ? `<span class="trait-qual dim">${esc(item.notes)}</span>` : ''}</div>` +
 2648:         `</div></div>`;
 2649:     }
 2650:   }
 2651: 
 2652:   // ── Armour ──
 2653:   if (combatArmour.length) {
 2654:     h += '<div class="sh-sub-title">Armour</div>';
 2655:     // Issue #879 (ADR-006 D2 + Concern #8): soft non-blocking hint when
 2656:     // multiple armour items are in state==='worn'. Stacking rule is
 2657:     // worst-case (Math.max of all defence_penalties); the hint exists so

 2662:     }
 2663:     const baseDefence = calcDefence(c);
 2664:     for (const { item, entry, idx } of combatArmour) {
 2665:       const name  = entry.name || item.catalogue_id;

 2679:         (qual || item.notes ? `<div class="trait-sub">${qual ? `<span class="trait-qual">${esc(qual)}</span>` : ''}${item.notes ? `<span class="trait-qual dim">${esc(item.notes)}</span>` : ''}</div>` : '') +

+# Adversarial review findings — issue #1154 / EQC-3
+
+## High
+
+- [Pass 1] None found.
+
+## Medium
+
+### [Pass 1] Uppercase 24-hex `container_id` values are rejected despite satisfying the stated shape
+
+- **Severity:** Medium
+- **File:line:** `server/routes/characters.js:879`
+- **Triggering input or sequence:** Send an otherwise-valid equipment-add request with `container_id` equal to an already-owned container catalogue ID rendered in uppercase hexadecimal, for example `ABCDEF0123456789ABCDEF01`.
+- **Observable consequence:** `ObjectId.isValid()` accepts the value, but `String(new ObjectId(value))` canonicalizes it to lowercase, so the strict equality check returns 400. That conflicts with the diff's stated contract of accepting a “24-hex ObjectId-shaped string,” for which uppercase hex is ordinarily shape-valid.
+- **Confidence:** Medium-high from the diff alone; Pass 2 must check whether the schema deliberately narrows the field to lowercase.
+
+## Low
+
+### [Pass 1] The self-reference guard relies on object identity and its test only exercises the favorable identity case
+
+- **Severity:** Low
+- **File:line:** `public/js/data/equipment-derivation.js:148`; `server/tests/issue-879-defence-penalty-wirein.test.js:578`
+- **Triggering input or sequence:** Call `equipmentContainerLabel()` with `item` as a clone/rebuilt object while `allEquipment` contains the original row with the same `catalogue_id`, where that ID also equals `item.container_id`.
+- **Observable consequence:** `e !== item` is true for the distinct objects, so the only row can be treated as “another” owned row and the item can be labelled as contained in itself. The new test uses `[item]`, so it cannot discriminate reference identity from stable row identity. Whether the production call can supply clones is deferred to Pass 2 repository tracing.
+- **Confidence:** High that the helper has this behavior; confidence that the current UI can trigger it is intentionally deferred until Pass 2.
+
+### [Pass 1] Rejection tests do not prove the advertised all-before-write behavior
+
+- **Severity:** Low
+- **File:line:** `server/tests/equipment.test.js:266`
+- **Triggering input or sequence:** Introduce a regression that pushes the requested equipment row and then returns one of the tested 400 responses for malformed, unowned, or non-container `container_id`.
+- **Observable consequence:** All three new rejection tests still pass because they assert only status/error/message and never reread the character to prove its equipment array stayed unchanged. The displayed route implementation writes only after validation, but the tests do not protect that load-bearing property against later refactoring.
+- **Confidence:** High.
+

+# Adversarial review findings — issue #1154 / EQC-3
+
+## High
+
+- [Pass 1] None found.
+
+## Medium
+
+### [Pass 1] Uppercase 24-hex `container_id` values are rejected despite satisfying the stated shape
+
+- **Severity:** Medium
+- **File:line:** `server/routes/characters.js:879`
+- **Triggering input or sequence:** Send an otherwise-valid equipment-add request with `container_id` equal to an already-owned container catalogue ID rendered in uppercase hexadecimal, for example `ABCDEF0123456789ABCDEF01`.
+- **Observable consequence:** `ObjectId.isValid()` accepts the value, but `String(new ObjectId(value))` canonicalizes it to lowercase, so the strict equality check returns 400. That conflicts with the diff's stated contract of accepting a “24-hex ObjectId-shaped string,” for which uppercase hex is ordinarily shape-valid.
+- **Confidence:** Medium-high from the diff alone; Pass 2 must check whether the schema deliberately narrows the field to lowercase.
+
+## Low
+
+### [Pass 1] The self-reference guard relies on object identity and its test only exercises the favorable identity case
+
+- **Severity:** Low
+- **File:line:** `public/js/data/equipment-derivation.js:148`; `server/tests/issue-879-defence-penalty-wirein.test.js:578`
+- **Triggering input or sequence:** Call `equipmentContainerLabel()` with `item` as a clone/rebuilt object while `allEquipment` contains the original row with the same `catalogue_id`, where that ID also equals `item.container_id`.
+- **Observable consequence:** `e !== item` is true for the distinct objects, so the only row can be treated as “another” owned row and the item can be labelled as contained in itself. The new test uses `[item]`, so it cannot discriminate reference identity from stable row identity. Whether the production call can supply clones is deferred to Pass 2 repository tracing.
+- **Confidence:** High that the helper has this behavior; confidence that the current UI can trigger it is intentionally deferred until Pass 2.
+
+### [Pass 1] Rejection tests do not prove the advertised all-before-write behavior
+
+- **Severity:** Low
+- **File:line:** `server/tests/equipment.test.js:266`
+- **Triggering input or sequence:** Introduce a regression that pushes the requested equipment row and then returns one of the tested 400 responses for malformed, unowned, or non-container `container_id`.
+- **Observable consequence:** All three new rejection tests still pass because they assert only status/error/message and never reread the character to prove its equipment array stayed unchanged. The displayed route implementation writes only after validation, but the tests do not protect that load-bearing property against later refactoring.
+- **Confidence:** High.
+

+# Adversarial review findings — issue #1154 / EQC-3
+
+## High
+
+- [Pass 1] None found.
+
+## Medium
+
+### [Pass 1] Uppercase 24-hex `container_id` values are rejected despite satisfying the stated shape
+
+- **Severity:** Medium
+- **File:line:** `server/routes/characters.js:879`
+- **Triggering input or sequence:** Send an otherwise-valid equipment-add request with `container_id` equal to an already-owned container catalogue ID rendered in uppercase hexadecimal, for example `ABCDEF0123456789ABCDEF01`.
+- **Observable consequence:** `ObjectId.isValid()` accepts the value, but `String(new ObjectId(value))` canonicalizes it to lowercase, so the strict equality check returns 400. That conflicts with the diff's stated contract of accepting a “24-hex ObjectId-shaped string,” for which uppercase hex is ordinarily shape-valid.
+- **Confidence:** Medium-high from the diff alone; Pass 2 must check whether the schema deliberately narrows the field to lowercase.
+
+## Low
+
+### [Pass 1] The self-reference guard relies on object identity and its test only exercises the favorable identity case
+
+- **Severity:** Low
+- **File:line:** `public/js/data/equipment-derivation.js:148`; `server/tests/issue-879-defence-penalty-wirein.test.js:578`
+- **Triggering input or sequence:** Call `equipmentContainerLabel()` with `item` as a clone/rebuilt object while `allEquipment` contains the original row with the same `catalogue_id`, where that ID also equals `item.container_id`.
+- **Observable consequence:** `e !== item` is true for the distinct objects, so the only row can be treated as “another” owned row and the item can be labelled as contained in itself. The new test uses `[item]`, so it cannot discriminate reference identity from stable row identity. Whether the production call can supply clones is deferred to Pass 2 repository tracing.
+- **Confidence:** High that the helper has this behavior; confidence that the current UI can trigger it is intentionally deferred until Pass 2.
+
+### [Pass 1] Rejection tests do not prove the advertised all-before-write behavior
+
+- **Severity:** Low
+- **File:line:** `server/tests/equipment.test.js:266`
+- **Triggering input or sequence:** Introduce a regression that pushes the requested equipment row and then returns one of the tested 400 responses for malformed, unowned, or non-container `container_id`.
+- **Observable consequence:** All three new rejection tests still pass because they assert only status/error/message and never reread the character to prove its equipment array stayed unchanged. The displayed route implementation writes only after validation, but the tests do not protect that load-bearing property against later refactoring.
+- **Confidence:** High.
+

+# Adversarial review findings — issue #1154 / EQC-3
+
+## High
+
+- [Pass 1] None found.
+
+## Medium
+
+### [Pass 1] Uppercase 24-hex `container_id` values are rejected despite satisfying the stated shape
+
+- **Severity:** Medium
+- **File:line:** `server/routes/characters.js:879`
+- **Triggering input or sequence:** Send an otherwise-valid equipment-add request with `container_id` equal to an already-owned container catalogue ID rendered in uppercase hexadecimal, for example `ABCDEF0123456789ABCDEF01`.
+- **Observable consequence:** `ObjectId.isValid()` accepts the value, but `String(new ObjectId(value))` canonicalizes it to lowercase, so the strict equality check returns 400. That conflicts with the diff's stated contract of accepting a “24-hex ObjectId-shaped string,” for which uppercase hex is ordinarily shape-valid.
+- **Confidence:** Medium-high from the diff alone; Pass 2 must check whether the schema deliberately narrows the field to lowercase.
+
+## Low
+
+### [Pass 1] The self-reference guard relies on object identity and its test only exercises the favorable identity case
+
+- **Severity:** Low
+- **File:line:** `public/js/data/equipment-derivation.js:148`; `server/tests/issue-879-defence-penalty-wirein.test.js:578`
+- **Triggering input or sequence:** Call `equipmentContainerLabel()` with `item` as a clone/rebuilt object while `allEquipment` contains the original row with the same `catalogue_id`, where that ID also equals `item.container_id`.
+- **Observable consequence:** `e !== item` is true for the distinct objects, so the only row can be treated as “another” owned row and the item can be labelled as contained in itself. The new test uses `[item]`, so it cannot discriminate reference identity from stable row identity. Whether the production call can supply clones is deferred to Pass 2 repository tracing.
+- **Confidence:** High that the helper has this behavior; confidence that the current UI can trigger it is intentionally deferred until Pass 2.
+
+### [Pass 1] Rejection tests do not prove the advertised all-before-write behavior
+
+- **Severity:** Low
+- **File:line:** `server/tests/equipment.test.js:266`
+- **Triggering input or sequence:** Introduce a regression that pushes the requested equipment row and then returns one of the tested 400 responses for malformed, unowned, or non-container `container_id`.
+- **Observable consequence:** All three new rejection tests still pass because they assert only status/error/message and never reread the character to prove its equipment array stayed unchanged. The displayed route implementation writes only after validation, but the tests do not protect that load-bearing property against later refactoring.
+- **Confidence:** High.
+

2026-08-13T04:39:27.926475Z ERROR codex_models_manager::manager: failed to renew cache TTL: missing field `base_instructions` at line 94 column 5
codex
Pass 2 repository tracing confirms the production renderer passes the same equipment object references, so the Pass 1 self-reference concern is not currently reachable there. It also reveals two concrete gaps: full character writes can bypass the new reference validation, and a container placed inside another container is accepted but never annotated in the Containers section. I’m freezing these repository-context findings before opening the story.

+# Adversarial review findings — issue #1154 / EQC-3
+
+## High
+
+- [Pass 1] None found.
+
+## Medium
+
+### [Pass 1] Uppercase 24-hex `container_id` values are rejected despite satisfying the stated shape
+
+- **Severity:** Medium
+- **File:line:** `server/routes/characters.js:879`
+- **Triggering input or sequence:** Send an otherwise-valid equipment-add request with `container_id` equal to an already-owned container catalogue ID rendered in uppercase hexadecimal, for example `ABCDEF0123456789ABCDEF01`.
+- **Observable consequence:** `ObjectId.isValid()` accepts the value, but `String(new ObjectId(value))` canonicalizes it to lowercase, so the strict equality check returns 400. That conflicts with the diff's stated contract of accepting a “24-hex ObjectId-shaped string,” for which uppercase hex is ordinarily shape-valid.
+- **Confidence:** Medium-high from the diff alone; Pass 2 must check whether the schema deliberately narrows the field to lowercase.
+
+### [Pass 2] Full-character create/replace paths bypass container ownership and bucket validation
+
+- **Severity:** Medium
+- **File:line:** `server/routes/characters.js:412`, `server/routes/characters.js:439`, `server/routes/characters.js:451`
+- **Triggering input or sequence:** Submit `equipment[]` through `POST /api/characters/wizard`, `POST /api/characters`, or especially the live full-document `PUT /api/characters/:id` path with a lowercase 24-hex `container_id` that is dangling or points to a non-container catalogue row. The normal admin Save-to-DB flow includes the complete `equipment` array in that PUT (`public/js/admin.js:964-990`).
+- **Observable consequence:** Schema validation checks only the string pattern and the PUT hydration loop checks only each row's `catalogue_id`; none of these paths performs the new same-character ownership or container-bucket checks. Invalid containment rejected by `POST /:id/equipment` can therefore be persisted through other real write paths, leaving enforcement dependent on which endpoint the caller used.
+- **Confidence:** High.
+
+### [Pass 2] Container-inside-container assignments are accepted but hidden in the Containers section
+
+- **Severity:** Medium
+- **File:line:** `public/js/editor/sheet.js:2755`, `public/js/editor/sheet.js:2799`; `server/routes/characters.js:905`
+- **Triggering input or sequence:** Own a Haven container, choose a Safe from the add form's Container bucket, select the Haven in “Place inside,” and add it. The picker is independent of the selected item bucket and the route validates only the target bucket, so this succeeds.
+- **Observable consequence:** The Safe's `container_id` is persisted, but the Containers renderer never calls `containedLabel(item)`, unlike the other six rendered sections. The sheet therefore gives no visible indication that the Safe is inside the Haven even though the UI offered and stored exactly that assignment.
+- **Confidence:** High.
+
+## Low
+
+### [Pass 1] The self-reference guard relies on object identity and its test only exercises the favorable identity case
+
+- **Severity:** Low
+- **File:line:** `public/js/data/equipment-derivation.js:148`; `server/tests/issue-879-defence-penalty-wirein.test.js:578`
+- **Triggering input or sequence:** Call `equipmentContainerLabel()` with `item` as a clone/rebuilt object while `allEquipment` contains the original row with the same `catalogue_id`, where that ID also equals `item.container_id`.
+- **Observable consequence:** `e !== item` is true for the distinct objects, so the only row can be treated as “another” owned row and the item can be labelled as contained in itself. The new test uses `[item]`, so it cannot discriminate reference identity from stable row identity. Whether the production call can supply clones is deferred to Pass 2 repository tracing.
+- **Confidence:** High that the helper has this behavior; confidence that the current UI can trigger it is intentionally deferred until Pass 2.
+
+### [Pass 1] Rejection tests do not prove the advertised all-before-write behavior
+
+- **Severity:** Low
+- **File:line:** `server/tests/equipment.test.js:266`
+- **Triggering input or sequence:** Introduce a regression that pushes the requested equipment row and then returns one of the tested 400 responses for malformed, unowned, or non-container `container_id`.
+- **Observable consequence:** All three new rejection tests still pass because they assert only status/error/message and never reread the character to prove its equipment array stayed unchanged. The displayed route implementation writes only after validation, but the tests do not protect that load-bearing property against later refactoring.
+- **Confidence:** High.
+
+### [Pass 2] Pass 1's uppercase-ID finding is contradicted by the repository schema
+
+- **Severity:** Low
+- **File:line:** `server/schemas/character.schema.js:362`
+- **Triggering input or sequence:** Compare the route's lowercase-canonical ObjectId check with the pre-existing `container_id` schema pattern.
+- **Observable consequence:** The schema explicitly permits only `^[a-f0-9]{24}$`, so rejecting uppercase is consistent with the repository's actual field contract. This removes the practical defect asserted in Pass 1, although that frozen finding remains above as required by the review protocol; the route/error wording “24-hex” is merely less precise than the schema.
+- **Confidence:** High.
+
+### [Pass 2] Pass 1's cloned-object self-reference concern is not reachable from the current renderer
+
+- **Severity:** Low
+- **File:line:** `public/js/editor/sheet.js:2582`, `public/js/editor/sheet.js:2610`, `public/js/editor/sheet.js:2604`
+- **Triggering input or sequence:** Trace an equipment row from `equip = c.equipment || []`, through `item = equip[i]`, into `byBucket[bucket].push({ item, ... })`, and then into `containedLabel(item)`.
+- **Observable consequence:** The production call retains the exact same object reference, so `e !== item` correctly excludes the row itself. A direct probe still returned `in: Self` for a cloned `item`, leaving the exported helper brittle for future callers, but the only current non-test caller cannot trigger it.
+- **Confidence:** High.
+
+### [Pass 2] “Lost” containers remain selectable and continue to count as owned
+
+- **Severity:** Low
+- **File:line:** `public/js/editor/sheet.js:2799`; `public/js/data/equipment-derivation.js:103`, `public/js/data/equipment-derivation.js:155`; `server/routes/characters.js:912`
+- **Triggering input or sequence:** Keep or add a container row with `state: 'lost'`, then use it as the “Place inside” target, or render an existing item whose `container_id` points to that lost row.
+- **Observable consequence:** Repository semantics explicitly describe `lost` as “the item is gone,” but the picker includes every container-bucket row and both server and label ownership checks ignore state. Users can place new items inside a gone container and existing contents continue to display as inside it.
+- **Confidence:** Medium-high; the code behavior and existing state semantics are clear, while the story may define “owns” structurally as row presence rather than possession state.
+
+### [Pass 2] Validation and mutation are vulnerable to a narrow delete race
+
+- **Severity:** Low
+- **File:line:** `server/routes/characters.js:902`, `server/routes/characters.js:946`, `server/routes/characters.js:962`
+- **Triggering input or sequence:** An add request reads a character that owns the target container and passes validation; before its `$push`, a concurrent request removes that container row.
+- **Observable consequence:** The add can persist an immediately dangling `container_id`. Conversely, the prompt's simultaneous “first request adds the container” scenario cannot incorrectly pass on a stale read—it can only reject until the first write becomes visible. This race is narrow for a single-ST-admin tool, but the validation and write are not atomic.
+- **Confidence:** High on the race mechanics; low-to-medium on operational likelihood.

+- **Triggering input or sequence:** Own a Haven container, choose a Safe from the add form's Container bucket, select the Haven in “Place inside,” and add it. The picker is independent of the selected item bucket and the route validates only the target bucket, so this succeeds.
+- **Observable consequence:** The Safe's `container_id` is persisted, but the Containers renderer never calls `containedLabel(item)`, unlike the other six rendered sections. The sheet therefore gives no visible indication that the Safe is inside the Haven even though the UI offered and stored exactly that assignment.
+- **Confidence:** High.
+
+## Low
+
+### [Pass 1] The self-reference guard relies on object identity and its test only exercises the favorable identity case
+
+- **Severity:** Low
+- **File:line:** `public/js/data/equipment-derivation.js:148`; `server/tests/issue-879-defence-penalty-wirein.test.js:578`
+- **Triggering input or sequence:** Call `equipmentContainerLabel()` with `item` as a clone/rebuilt object while `allEquipment` contains the original row with the same `catalogue_id`, where that ID also equals `item.container_id`.
+- **Observable consequence:** `e !== item` is true for the distinct objects, so the only row can be treated as “another” owned row and the item can be labelled as contained in itself. The new test uses `[item]`, so it cannot discriminate reference identity from stable row identity. Whether the production call can supply clones is deferred to Pass 2 repository tracing.
+- **Confidence:** High that the helper has this behavior; confidence that the current UI can trigger it is intentionally deferred until Pass 2.
+
+### [Pass 1] Rejection tests do not prove the advertised all-before-write behavior
+
+- **Severity:** Low
+- **File:line:** `server/tests/equipment.test.js:266`
+- **Triggering input or sequence:** Introduce a regression that pushes the requested equipment row and then returns one of the tested 400 responses for malformed, unowned, or non-container `container_id`.
+- **Observable consequence:** All three new rejection tests still pass because they assert only status/error/message and never reread the character to prove its equipment array stayed unchanged. The displayed route implementation writes only after validation, but the tests do not protect that load-bearing property against later refactoring.
+- **Confidence:** High.
+
+### [Pass 2] Pass 1's uppercase-ID finding is contradicted by the repository schema
+
+- **Severity:** Low
+- **File:line:** `server/schemas/character.schema.js:362`
+- **Triggering input or sequence:** Compare the route's lowercase-canonical ObjectId check with the pre-existing `container_id` schema pattern.
+- **Observable consequence:** The schema explicitly permits only `^[a-f0-9]{24}$`, so rejecting uppercase is consistent with the repository's actual field contract. This removes the practical defect asserted in Pass 1, although that frozen finding remains above as required by the review protocol; the route/error wording “24-hex” is merely less precise than the schema.
+- **Confidence:** High.
+
+### [Pass 2] Pass 1's cloned-object self-reference concern is not reachable from the current renderer
+
+- **Severity:** Low
+- **File:line:** `public/js/editor/sheet.js:2582`, `public/js/editor/sheet.js:2610`, `public/js/editor/sheet.js:2604`
+- **Triggering input or sequence:** Trace an equipment row from `equip = c.equipment || []`, through `item = equip[i]`, into `byBucket[bucket].push({ item, ... })`, and then into `containedLabel(item)`.
+- **Observable consequence:** The production call retains the exact same object reference, so `e !== item` correctly excludes the row itself. A direct probe still returned `in: Self` for a cloned `item`, leaving the exported helper brittle for future callers, but the only current non-test caller cannot trigger it.
+- **Confidence:** High.
+
+### [Pass 2] “Lost” containers remain selectable and continue to count as owned
+
+- **Severity:** Low
+- **File:line:** `public/js/editor/sheet.js:2799`; `public/js/data/equipment-derivation.js:103`, `public/js/data/equipment-derivation.js:155`; `server/routes/characters.js:912`
+- **Triggering input or sequence:** Keep or add a container row with `state: 'lost'`, then use it as the “Place inside” target, or render an existing item whose `container_id` points to that lost row.
+- **Observable consequence:** Repository semantics explicitly describe `lost` as “the item is gone,” but the picker includes every container-bucket row and both server and label ownership checks ignore state. Users can place new items inside a gone container and existing contents continue to display as inside it.
+- **Confidence:** Medium-high; the code behavior and existing state semantics are clear, while the story may define “owns” structurally as row presence rather than possession state.
+
+### [Pass 2] Validation and mutation are vulnerable to a narrow delete race
+
+- **Severity:** Low
+- **File:line:** `server/routes/characters.js:902`, `server/routes/characters.js:946`, `server/routes/characters.js:962`
+- **Triggering input or sequence:** An add request reads a character that owns the target container and passes validation; before its `$push`, a concurrent request removes that container row.
+- **Observable consequence:** The add can persist an immediately dangling `container_id`. Conversely, the prompt's simultaneous “first request adds the container” scenario cannot incorrectly pass on a stale read—it can only reject until the first write becomes visible. This race is narrow for a single-ST-admin tool, but the validation and write are not atomic.
+- **Confidence:** High on the race mechanics; low-to-medium on operational likelihood.
